# QUEUE.md — PitchNotes-iOS task board

> The ONLY task board (AGENTS.md §2). Every agent: edit ONLY your own section,
> append to LOG (newest at TOP). Queue edits are tiny standalone commits
> (`queue: <claim/done/build> <task-id>`) — never bundled with code, pushed immediately.

Task row format (one line per task, keep it short):

```
- [P-014] DOING | swiftf0 ONNX spike on macOS runner | branch:paul | dep:— | build:<run-url>@<sha> | ac:spike asserts pitch+confidence on 3 fixtures | note:<handoff/next-step>
```

- IDs: `P-###` Paul, `J-###` Jess, `L-###` Leto, `X-###` cross-cutting (Leto assigns owner).
- Status lifecycle: `TODO → DOING → REVIEW → DONE`, plus `BLOCKED(reason)`;
  `BUILD_PENDING` is a *flag on the row*, never a reason to stop working.
- `ac:` = acceptance criteria. A task without `ac:` is not claimable; write one first.
- `note:` = handoff. EVERY finished task ends with a note telling the next reader
  exactly what exists, where, and what's next.
- Max one DOING coding task per agent; unlimited pending CI builds (tracked on the
  row via `build:`, not in your head).

---

The @5258632 run is the judge (in flight at the handoff). **THEN @5258632 run 45094828320 RED AGAIN — ONE error left:** `ALACStemWriter.swift:111: error: cannot convert value of type 'AVAudioPCMBuffer' to expected argument type 'CMSampleBuffer'` — `AVAssetWriterInput.append` on this SDK takes a **CMSampleBuffer**, not an AVAudioPCMBuffer (I wrote the AVAudioPCMBuffer overload from memory; it does not exist). TWO candidate fixes (pick one; CI is the judge, and Linux can verify NEITHER — `swiftc -parse` only): **(A) preferred — drop AVAssetWriter entirely and use `AVAudioFile(forWriting:settings:commonFormat:interleaved:)` + `write(from:)`** (~15 lines: settings `{AVFormatIDKey: kAudioFormatAppleLossless, AVSampleRateKey: 44100, AVNumberOfChannelsKey: channels, AVEncoderBitDepthHintKey: <bit depth>}`, feed the source's chunks; note the appended buffer must be in the OUTPUT file's processingFormat — create it with the same commonFormat as the buffer you feed). **(B) keep AVAssetWriter and wrap each chunk into a CMSampleBuffer** (`CMAudioFormatDescription` from the source `AVAudioFormat` + `CMSampleBufferCreate` with an `AudioBufferList` built from the buffer's channel planes — the deinterleaved source mixes naturally with it). Either way keep the existing assertions (lossless round-trip test asserts max|Δ| < 1.5/32767; the engine tests assert the bundle validates).** **REMAINING:** (1) CI verdict for the Apple-side code at @5258632 (X-204 step); (2) "sizes logged (~5–6 MB/min/stem)" — the writer does not log sizes yet; (3) the cache index is engine-side only — if Leto meant D-4's library index, that is Jess's J-202 and needs a ruling. **ALAC WRITER REWRITTEN ON `AVAudioFile` (fix A, @9e28910 pushed; CI run 45140277183 in flight):** the `AVAssetWriterInput.append(CMSampleBuffer)` route is GONE — the writer now uses `AVAudioFile(forWriting:settings:commonFormat:.pcmFormatFloat32,interleaved:false)` (the ALAC encoder's only accepted layout) and streams 8 192-frame chunks into it (per-plane float32 copy; both sides deinterleaved), finalising the `.m4a` by releasing the file inside a nested scope BEFORE the size is measured and before the orchestrator validates/reads it (the old AVAssetWriter path finalised at `finishWriting`, the new one at dealloc — both happen before the call returns). **P-204's "sizes logged" ac leg is NOW DONE:** `encodeStemWithInfo` returns `EncodedStemInfo{part, channels, bytes, durationSeconds, megabytesPerMinute}` (logged via os.Logger, subsystem com.thesagezm.pitchnotes / category import) and the new macOS test `testALACStemWriterReportsEncodedSize` asserts the report matches the file on disk (bytes>0, duration 1.0 s, finite MB/min). NOTE on the §8 figure: 5–6 MB/min is for REAL music — a synthetic sine compresses far below it, so the test asserts the SIZE REPORT, not the §8 ratio (the ratio is checked against a real import at P-603). **RUNTIME DEFECTS FIXED @b7aecc2 (run 45153726873 IN FLIGHT) — CI run 45143126945@afd1080 was RED on TWO runtime defects, NOT on compile (the module and the tests compile and run):** **(A) the ALAC encode failed at runtime in all three ALAC tests** with ONE opaque error ("The operation couldn't be completed. (Foundation._GenericObjCError error 0.)") — the outer catch swallowed WHICH call failed, so the message said nothing. FIX: the writer now runs a STRATEGY LADDER and reports PER-STEP diagnostics (open / read / copy / write) with the NSError's domain, code and userInfo; every attempt's failure is collected and the aggregate error names them all, so ONE CI round trip is decisive. The ladder covers the two causes that could not be ruled out from Linux (the ALAC encoder is Apple-only): (a) the `AVEncoderBitDepthHintKey` hint CONTRADICTING the float32 client format (hint and no-hint variants), and (b) the `read(into:frameCount:)` overload refusing a SHORT final read (a 44 100-frame source ends on a partial 3 140-frame chunk — the older `read(into:)` overload, the one the decoder uses, is the alternative); plus the int16 client layout. Each attempt opens a FRESH source file (a failed attempt leaves the read position mid-file). The winning strategy is logged. **(B) the two engine tests threw a MASKING error** — "Error Domain=NSCocoaErrorDomain Code=4 "“.work” couldn’t be removed." (NSFilePath=<test-temp>/library/.work, underlying POSIX ENOENT) — which REPLACED the real ALAC failure. The only ".work" path in the tree is the engine's work dir (libraryRoot/.work, created ONLY by the standalone `importAudio` flow — the import path never creates it), and removing a path that was never created throws. FIX (both directions): the engine's deinit removes the work dir ONLY if it EXISTS and swallows+logs any failure (never throws); `ImportOrchestrator` gained `removeIfPresent` (idempotent, never throws — the failure is RETURNED as a message) and `preserving` (the ORIGINAL error's type and message survive; a cleanup note is APPENDED, never substituted), so **a failed import surfaces the REAL error**. 4 new Linux tests cover the cleanup helpers (EngineCore suite). **REMAINING:** (1) the CI verdict for @b7aecc2 (X-204 step — the judge; if the ALAC write still fails, the aggregate error now names the exact step and cause); (2) the cache index is engine-side only — if Leto meant D-4's library index, that is Jess's J-202 and needs a ruling. **DONE (queue): run 45156291152@1935a0f GREEN 8/8 — the ALAC writer WORKS at runtime (all three ALAC tests pass, incl. the LOSSLESS round-trip: max|Δ| < 1.5/32767), the two engine tests pass with the REAL error surfacing (no more '.work' masking), the size report matches the file on disk, the bundle validates V1–V15, and EngineCore is 191/191 (187 + the 4 new cleanup tests). Every ac leg is met: ALAC decodes natively ✓, the bundle validates against CONTRACTS ✓, sizes are logged/reported ✓ (§8's 5–6 MB/min ratio is checked against a REAL import at P-603, not against a synthetic sine). STILL OPEN (not mine): the engine-side cache index is NOT D-4's library index (that is Jess's J-202) — a ruling is needed only if the app needs a different shape. FOLLOW-UP (next session, cheap): the strategy ladder's winner is now REPORTED (`EncodedStemInfo.strategy` + a print in the macOS test) — once the winning strategy is recorded, the ladder can be trimmed to that ONE strategy.**
